ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e2557	Federal Minister! My question is: [[/PAKT/VHG/XX/M/M_00005/index.shtml -- 5/M]] Will Austria be able to maintain its anonymity in the case of savings books?
ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e2584	Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e2599	Deputies! I hope so, for good legal reasons. Austria has always taken the fight against money laundering very seriously. As Foreign Minister, I have proposed to the House two international conventions on ratification these days, which are intended to take this fight against drug money and money laundering very seriously. We have presented to Parliament a provision that by 1. There are no longer any anonymous securities accounts to be set up on July 1996, and since 1987, then amended and improved 1989 and 1992, a so-called due diligence statement for all Austrian credit institutions, that, first, in the case of foreign currency accounts, each of them has to be legitimated in the case of more than 200 000 S, and that only the slightest suspicion must be reported immediately to the authorities. We therefore believe that we have done all we can to prove that we take seriously the rightful fight against money laundering, and I think there is no legal and no political argument at all to bring about a change in the 25 million anonymous savings books that the Austrians have and which are not at all suitable for money laundering, for the whitewashing of any drug money.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e2623	Additional question? - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e2638	Federal Minister! There have nevertheless been considerable differences of opinion within the Federal Government. No discussions on the maintenance of anonymity on June 1994?
ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e2656	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e2672	There is, of course, freedom of expression. Everyone can have their opinion. I have reflected the line of the Federal Government here, and it is, I believe, legally and politically well secured. By the way, for a few days now, there has been an own opinion by university professor Markus Achatz on the compatibility of Austrian anonymity with the European Union's money laundering directive. In this, our position is actually legally reflected. In the end, the European Court of Justice will rule in the event of a legal dispute, as has already been the case in hundreds of other cases. That's nothing special. I think we can argue. There are specific opinions, which are clear, which can also be different, it is important that we represent a clear line towards Brussels and towards the European Court of Justice.

My question to you: What do you think about strengthening the European Union's ability to act by returning to the national states, to the national parliaments, and to the national parliaments, in line with the principle of subsidiarity, and restricting itself to a few areas, but with concrete means of intervention and decision-making?
ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e3928	Please, Mr. Vice-Chancellor.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e3943	I believe that the concept of subsidiarity, i.e. the shift of responsibility to the respective level – that does not have to be the national level at all – can be very important to the countries, that can be the communities. This has also been strongly supported by the former President of the Commission, Delors, and – taken up by his successor Santer – has been made a central issue. The successes have so far been modest, and this must also be acknowledged openly. We Austrians are fighting for this subsidiarity, we are fighting for concreteization, i.e. for the criteria by which such a thing is made, so that one can verify afterwards: Did something really go on? We also want to discuss the methods in the Treaty in connection with the Intergovernmental Conference. We are also the only country that has so far worked effectively and in favour of strengthening the Committee of the Regions. But I would like to add another thought you have not mentioned: It is also important to me that we come more and more to majority voting. Not in the very sensitive areas, such as the choice of energy sources, space and land use or water resources, there remains the unanimity, the same with financial resources or with military security, that is clear, but there are so many areas in which majority decisions can be more achieved, so that I also think this issue is very important.

But I believe that the Intergovernmental Conference will go in this direction: humanitarian aid, crisis management, peace building, peace enforcement within the framework of the WEU. The EU as a political group gives the mandates, the WEU implements them, and Austria has undertaken to participate in this common policy from the outset in the framework of the Coalition Agreement with all its rights and obligations.

Point Report of the Justice Committee on the Government Template (102 of the Supplements): Federal Law on cooperation with the International Courts (154 of the Supplements)
ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e5703	We'll get to the 1st. Agenda item. This is the report of the Committee on Justice on the Government Template concerning a Federal Law on Cooperation with International Courts. The rapporteur is Mr Grossruck. I ask him to initiate the debate.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e5721	Mr President. High house! I am reporting from the Committee on Justice on the Government Template concerning a federal law on cooperation with the International Courts. The United Nations Security Council has established as a measure in the event of a threat or breach of peace under Chapter VII of the United Nations Statute with its Resolutions 827 and 955 International Courts for the former Yugoslavia and Rwanda. These measures are binding on all States and require them to cooperate with the International Courts. In order to fully comply with the general and special cooperation obligations, a legal basis is necessary. In order to fulfil the obligation of the Republic of Austria from the above-mentioned resolutions, the introduction of a federal law on cooperation with the International Courts is proposed. As a result of its deliberations, the Committee on Justice requests that the National Council give constitutional approval to the draft law (102 of the Supplements) presented by the Federal Government with the amendment to the written committee report. Mr President. I am trying to continue the debate.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e5748	I would like to thank the rapporteur for his statement. For this debate, I would like to remind you once again: First speakers 30 minutes, ÖVP and SPÖ voluntarily 20 minutes, all other speakers 10 minutes. I call Mr Vanner.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e5776	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! To the top I put the explanation: Harald Ofner: five minutes voluntarily. Under the title "Cooperation with International Courts", the issue is, among other things, the extradition of persons suspected of war crimes to such international courts, including the extradition of persons from states whose nationals they are. That is to say, it can be said that Austrian citizens who are suspected of having committed war crimes are also being extradited to other state areas, to the territories of other countries. I am in favour of the persecution of war criminals. I am in favour of their being condemned if the evidence to be dealt with adds that they can be shown that they have actually committed the crimes. There is, however, an ancient and hitherto unshakable principle in Austria that Austrians are to be punished in accordance with the principle of universality for criminal acts they have committed somewhere in the world, but in Austria and only in Austria if they stay in Austria. To extradite Austrians abroad for the purpose of persecuting for criminal acts is unusual, is not practised, has not yet been covered by law and law, and it seems to me to be of concern to law. I would like to stress that I am not speaking to my group as a whole. I am concerned about my personal experiences and about my personal views as a lawyer, as a lawyer and also as a judicial politician if you like. I believe that we should ensure that criminals – including war criminals – are punished, but that we should try to ensure that this is always done in countries where they are at home, if they are caught there, elsewhere, if they are caught elsewhere, and that the respective legal, criminal structures are put in place in those states, which also enables them to do so, ladies and gentlemen! [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] What things look like in practice has already been recognized: A Serbian officer has been extradited from a state-like structure, which has already been said on the occasion of his extradition: He's so sick, he won't sit long, and you won't be able to take a trial. It was General Djukic. At that time I thought it would be a great wonder – I don't want to put this right, but it would be very surprising to me – if you hadn't had assurances before that he won't sit long. Actually, it was. He died in the meantime. We were able to see in the media images on which the delivered, but then already dead, Djukic is transported in the coffin. The most important coffin on the right is another war criminal whose extradition is requested, Mladic. But he's not sick, he really has to have a procedure done over himself, and therefore he's not being extradited either. He wears the coffin of the then delivered and now dead. This shows how questionable such provisions are. I believe that they should not be decided as alibi treatments against all the principles in criminal matters. I repeat it again: Whoever commits crimes is to be punished. But it is an Austrian principle in criminal law that Austrians are brought to justice in Austria – no matter where they have employed something – and that they are not delivered to other countries for the purpose of prosecution. I therefore have personal, legal, political concerns about the provisions contained in the present proposal in this direction. I shall vote against it. It will not all be the same to me from my group.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e6126	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! Ladies and gentlemen! The rule of law is clear: The abandonment of criminal proceedings by the Republic of Austria, as it does according to § 4 para. 2 of the Government's proposal, is nothing but the partial task of a sovereign claim of a State. For criminal law still applies the principle of universality, which is based on the fact that the Republic of Austria raises a criminal claim and a criminal law claim in respect of all crimes, no matter at which angle of the earth these crimes were committed. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! On the other hand, however, I do not understand the commitments that the Republic of Austria has assumed through its accession to the United Nations. The Charter of the United Nations provides for the establishment of international courts in the event of serious violations of international humanitarian law. In other words, the International Court, which has now been set up to deal with the terrible crimes in the former Yugoslavia, is legally legitimate. Ladies and gentlemen! You know, in the past there has been some kind of tribunal that had no such well-founded legal basis. The current International Court, which is now being set up, has this legal basis under the Charter of the United Nations. After a very careful consideration between these two Poles and the special tension between the partial task of sovereignty, namely the abandonment of the right of enforcement of the Republic of Austria, and the obligations of the State community, I have come to the conclusion that I am in favour of taking back the Republic of Austria, both in the interest of the international community and in the interest of being able to condemn serious criminals who have violated international humanitarian law. Ladies and gentlemen! I believe that this government template and the provisions on the establishment of the International Court should also be seen in the sense of at least an attempt at war prophylaxis. For those who have closely followed the news broadcasts of the past few weeks will have realized that the fact of the establishment of the International Court and the fact that the Bosnian Serb leader Radovan Karadýi% is being searched for in writing, but had a political impact on its status in the Serbian part of Bosnia, although it is now obviously only taken back to the outside. ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e6268	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen. We also support this bill. I would like to take the opportunity to say a few more words in addition to what some of my previous speakers have already mentioned. We believe that the aggressment of war crimes is a very important prerequisite for a peace process, as it is now under attack in our neighbouring country, in the former Yugoslavia. Several human rights organizations and activists have repeatedly pointed out the importance of the atrocity of war crimes, the persecution of war criminals themselves for building civil life, but also for the credibility of the rule of law. All of this, in turn, is a prerequisite for the restoration of a democratic state and a civil life. But there is also the legitimate hope that such agreements, such international courts, such war tribunals, will also have a preventive character for all possible future cases, that it will be clear that war crimes are just being committed, and that they will not be unheard of. On this occasion, allow me to observe and add one more thing: However, we should not go so far as to believe that all these problems are now eliminated by this regulation in the case of ex-Yugoslavia and Rwanda and that this alone has done enough with regard to the war crimes complex. For here there is still a great danger of double morality; a danger that Austria has also been faced over the past two or three years. That's the side of the bend: There are also the victims of war criminals. There are the countless victims, especially the raped women. And what happens to those at the borders who fled, for example, to Austria? What do our asylum procedures look like? What does the permeability of the Austrian state look like here? That is, it is just as important to ask the question: How can provision be made at this level too? What about the deserters, those young men who do not want to take part in war, these abominations and crimes and see no other way of avoiding them than to flee? These have not received any legal status in an asylum procedure at all in Austria. But again to the raped women. In these days, we have received through the media the drafts for a new asylum law. I have great concerns and I see great dangers when, as we read, these asylum procedures are to be dealt with at the border in the future, as in this case with refugees of war and, above all, with the victims of such war crimes, or as their cases are treated. In Austrian criminal law, there is the possibility for women who have become victims of such crimes who have become victims of rapes to be a separate procedure. At least this should be the status of those women who flee the horrors from the crimes of war, that they should not be subjected to any unworthy procedure at the border, that they should not be detained in specially built camps until their asylum proceedings are decided upon. If we agree with such a government proposal, if we do so in the case of ex-Yugoslavia and Rwanda, we should remember, as I have said, that there are unfortunately other cases, other countries where there are civil war-like states in which there are wars which are not covered by this law, neither the persecution of war criminals nor the victims of them. I appeal to you if we are going to discuss the new asylum procedure over the next few weeks, to take into account this aspect of war crimes and to include them in the preliminary work on the Committee.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e6341	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Even though in the past all states have repeatedly claimed that genocide and other crimes against humanity are threatened with severe punishment even according to their law and they will either extradite or prosecute suspects themselves, in many cases an actual prosecution of these perpetrators by their national courts has not taken place. It was the terriblely painful experience of the recent past in South-East Europe, but also in Africa that the international community finally reached an agreement on the remorse of these crimes and could for the first time be established in the history of the United Nations two international courts. Here, not winners will judge the defeated, but as an ultimate ratio, the international community exercises criminal law. Those responsible for these crimes, in whatever positions they have or are in, must expect the Community of States to not rest until the international courts have judged them. This will certainly not miss a preventive effect against violations of international humanitarian law for the future. It will depend on the broadest support of all states whether the International Courts will prove themselves, be able to fulfil their task free of political influences and thus also play an impact on the future. This consideration is also based on the present draft law. We have largely refrained from formalities and conditions for cooperation with the International Court of Justice in the implementation of Austria and have also allowed it to conduct a certain independent survey in Austria. However, where fundamental rights of the individual or overarching interests of Austria are affected, the traditional legal aid path must be pursued. Experience with the International Court has already shown that this is in particular an effort to comply with the principles of the fair procedure. We were therefore able to give up with good conscience the otherwise usual extradition procedure and the otherwise usual reasons for the rejection of extradition between the States. In particular, it is also reasonable to extradite Austrian citizens to the International Court for prosecution, apart from the fact that the United Nations Security Council's resolutions leave no doubt that there is also an obligation under international law to transfer its own citizens and that, without their fulfilment, the whole project remains ineffective. Ladies and gentlemen! By setting up these two international courts for the former Yugoslavia and Rwanda, the efforts which have been in place for years to set up a permanent criminal court have received new impetus, although the conclusion of these works and the entry into force of a corresponding Convention are unfortunately not foreseeable. In the future, when it comes to exhorting others to comply with their obligations of cooperation with the International Courts, Austria must be able to point out and can do so under this law, that it has fully supported and will continue to support the idea of the international persecution of crimes against humanity. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e6511	I would like to thank the rapporteur for his statement. For this debate, it was established that the first speaker of each group will not be more than 30 minutes, who will not speak more than 10 minutes. Mr Krüger spoke. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e6539	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The adoption of the Government template, together with the amendments tabled concerning the EU Company Law Change Act, has preceded a wide-ranging review process. The need for such a law is not very recent. A few years ago, the Re-establishment Tax Act was created in order to favour the re-establishment of social law. At that time, however, only the tax conditions were created which essentially consisted in the fact that there is not a so-called liquidation stock in the event of a re-establishment, which leads to the taxation of all silent reserves. Now, in part, the commercial law is being implemented, which has been created taxly by the Re-establishment Tax Act a few years ago. I am thinking, for example, of the plant breakdown and the like. As a result, this EU company law amendment law is to be welcomed from my point of view, because it takes into account the needs of companies in Austria, such as the need for a so-called Einmann GmbH. The approach to the law was that a GmbH is first and foremost a company and that a company can logically only exist if there are at least two shareholders. In practice, this obstacle to a former Einmann-GmbH has been overcome by the fact that a so-called founding helper has occurred when a limited-liability company was established, often also in the person of a notary or lawyer or a tax advisor who has entered into the company contract with the main shareholder and has, after the registration of the company with limited liability, returned to the company book by a contract of assignment. This operation was naturally cost-related. However, I believe that if a legal order is committed to enabling companies with limited liability and also to favouring fiscal, as it has sometimes occurred, then one must consistently confess that even an individual entrepreneur, i.e. an sole shareholder, acts in a legal and business manner in the legal form of a GmbH. Ladies and gentlemen! Legally speaking, that is not entirely inconceivable. We know from practice that many companies with limited liability are only established for the purpose and that the liability of the individual partner is limited to the parent capital, is limited to the business deposit and there is no personal liability. However, in the past this has led, as we know, to the founding of small companies with limited liability, which in turn has been followed by an insolvency. In order to counter this fact, an increased publicity has now been introduced by the EU Company Change Act. This increased publicity has therefore been introduced because there is a European Union directive to be implemented internally. I believe that this directive also has legal rights, because, of course, increased publicity must also be seen from the point of view of insolvency. However, it is not to be explored that compliance with the needs of the economy after increased publicity may be associated with an increase in costs, but in any case with greater accounting effort for each company. However, according to the new EU Company Law Change Act, which is pending today, so-called small companies with limited liability would be obliged to publish fully. In other words, according to the provisions of the Law on limited liability companies, they would be obliged to draw up the annual accounts not only within five months with a subsequent short extension period, but also to submit it to the Court of Justice for publication within a total period of nine months. This is certainly not a cure, it is not a suitable insolvency prophylaxis, because – I know, paper is patient – the approaches that can be used for an annual report are naturally very different. It is also true that, according to the bill, the tax balance must be submitted and not for example a asset status which would clearly have the advantage that silent reserves should be taken into account in the assets, but also in the liabilities. But it would be an unreasonable effort – that is not a question at all – if one were now to make a provision according to which every company, even a small company, would not only have to submit a fiscal annual accounts, but would also have to submit a wealth status. Ladies and gentlemen! This issue inevitably leads me to discuss improvements in insolvency rights. In recent days, the social partners have tabled a proposal to reform insolvency rights. I would remind you that the Chamber of Commerce and the Chamber of Labour have made a common proposal. This was critically appreciated in the media. Ladies and gentlemen! I do not want to hide my clear position on this proposal from the point of view of the practitioner. I believe that my opinion is also partly shared by the Minister of Justice and the officials in the Ministry of Justice, but also by the head of the credit protection association. Maybe not in this drastic view of things: I am of the opinion that, ladies and gentlemen, this proposal by the social partners, which should be reformed, is a smooth humbug, because this proposal by the social partners is in line with the introduction of some sort of secret remediation procedure at court, to which only the court, a reorganisation officer and banks should be allowed, but not the suppliers. So it is all to be done that this process of rehabilitation is almost done under the spell. It is a political in the right that a group, namely the suppliers, should be completely eliminated! Remember, for example, that a supplier makes a credit request to a bank that itself is a affected person. Or remember that a credit rating request is made at a credit protection association, the KSV or the AKV, which, of course, needs to know about these so-called secret procedures. It would then, logically, be obliged to deny the fact of a reorganisation procedure against its members and its suppliers legally legitimated. This is an unsustainable state! Who makes such a bill? This is a lay theatre: it is assumed that a court procedure is to be partially secret! But there is something to be said about it, which is not the only nonsense attached to this proposal by the social partners. Consideration to initiate a reorganisation procedure forcefully if the equity ratio falls below 5 percent or if the equity ratio that has moved between 5 and 10 percent falls by 2 percent in recent years is also a complete nonsense. If this were to be used as a basis for rehabilitation or as a reason for initiating a judicial rehabilitation procedure, then 10,000 companies in Austria would have to enter into this rehabilitation procedure immediately, I guarantee you. The courts would be overwhelmed that the economic cycle would collapse. I guarantee you this, I know that from my experience in practice, and ladies and gentlemen from the economic professions will be right to say that very, very many, thousands of companies in Austria do not have an equity ratio of 10 percent. I also do not see the schematic view of this attitude, this 10 percent. I have already said that on the one hand there is an annual accounts which take account of the requirement of the Federal Tax Code and on the other hand a commercial status, and these two balance sheets, namely tax and a kind of realistic trade balance within the meaning of status, can vary considerably. I guarantee you: If the tax balance were to be used today as a parameter for the initiation of such a procedure, thousands of companies would be immediately affected by this reorganisation procedure. On the other hand, if we were to say that the tax balance has nothing or very little to do with the economic situation of a company, every company, including a small company, must be required to establish a status, to carry out a resolution of the silent reserves, to evaluate each individual economic asset not according to the accounting and financial value, but according to the actual economic value. That would be an enormous effort. In addition, this would certainly contribute to further dilution of the capital cap and further dilution of liquidity. That too is complete nonsense! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! We of the liberals have also thought about how to really improve insolvency law. There are some approaches to this, such as the indisputability of rehabilitation loans. The practice in insolvency law is that the banks no longer grant loans for restructuring, because the bank directors and lawyers believe that they will soon be charged with the aid for negligent Krida or bankruptcy aid. They lose the nerves, as was the case with Maculan, and turn to the credit tap, although they would in principle be willing to continue to finance after a sober analysis and dive through the sole of the valley. Therefore, it would be important to modify the right of challenge in such a way that, as it is known, it is also regulated in the bankruptcy order, loans granted for the renovation of a beaten company could no longer be challenged in the sense of the general faith and that bank managers would then be forced to turn to the credit tap according to the principle of prudent attitude – one can also call it cowardice. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] In my opinion, another point of view would be to create a uniform insolvency procedure, and I also believe that my group would be in favour of creating a single insolvency procedure. I quite agree that the Chamber of Labour is also proposing a uniform insolvency procedure, because I am on the view that not everything must be nonsense, which comes from the Chamber of Workers. There should no longer be any distinction between a compensatory procedure and a bankruptcy procedure, but there should be a uniform insolvency procedure, a uniform insolvency stock. In my opinion, this would have the advantage of creating a disstigmatisation of the bankruptcys, because the word "discourse" in Austria means the entire economic apprehension of an act, and that is mostly the beginning of the end. In addition, the bankruptcy is almost automatically linked to a closure or closure, because no one could take the risk of rehabilitation. We are therefore in favour of a uniform insolvency procedure, and the limitations of the managing directors should then be determined in individual cases. Further approach in the context of a single insolvency procedure: The compensatory rate should be reduced. They know that there is a minimum rate of 40 per cent in the compensatory procedure and that a 20 per cent minimum rate exists in the compensating procedure, i.e. in that procedure, which is then carried out in the course of bankruptcy proceedings. It would be useful to create a uniform compensation rate of 30 percent in a single insolvency procedure. [[Abg. Dr. Fekter: Why should this be uniform?]] Mrs Fekter! We can talk about it elsewhere – from the practitioner to the theorist – and I will explain it to you. Ladies and gentlemen! Another point is the introduction of an analogy to the Chapter Eleven. We know from American insolvency law that this standard, namely Chapter Eleven, has proved itself. It is about the fact that a beaten company is given a certain, short-term legal moratorium and that within this open moratorium – and not, for example, under the virtue of the social partners – within this legal moratorium the speu separates itself from the wheat and the company is either re-trained or finally abandoned to the ruin. In my opinion, there is another nonsense in insolvency law, or in the secondary laws of insolvency law, in the administration and in the legalisation of the pleas fund. A few days ago, Mr Vavrovsky, a Salzburg lawyer specializing in insolvency law, was questioned, and his opinion was that when he was a compensation manager at Hallein, the rates of the Mercedes purchases of the local Hallein Mercedes dealer increased dramatically. What is this due to? - To the double reference. And that's really not to be seen! We liberals believe that the demands of workers must not be reduced in any way. But it is not to be seen that if I am today a worker of a company who becomes insolvent, I early retirement from that company, take the entire compensation for dismissal, enter the next day into the collecting company and involve a double salary. This is nonsense, ladies and gentlemen, which is still being eliminated! [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] Finally, I should like to point out that, in principle, we are in favour of the EU-Society Change Act by the liberals, that we will essentially agree with this decision-making, but, of course, as we have done in committee, we are asking for a separate vote on paragraph 20, because this paragraph 20 provides for a restriction on the distribution. This is a standard that had to be introduced in order to secure the Structural Adjustment Act by commercial law. – I think it is completely nonsense to start with some tax law standard, which has nothing to do with business conditions, and then introduce a commercial standard artificially. You will not find our approval, ladies and gentlemen. That is only logical, because if we have voted against the Structural Adjustment Act with good reason, we will certainly not agree on this point. We shall therefore vote against this point in the separate vote.

So it will also need that of a period of habit. I would therefore ask you to be really careful in dealing with penalties in this respect. In addition, I would like to ask all the colleagues in this House to consider together the practice of this obligation to deposit small Gesmbhs and the effect that it is intended to produce – whether it brings anything at all – and perhaps also in a two-year observation period, whether it was really useful. If we come to the conclusion that what may be the only thought behind it, namely, the protection of creditors or the prevention of insolvency, is not given by this deposit, then I would suggest that we should sit down and think about whether such a paragraph, which has no practical effect, might be completely deleted again. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] As a third point, I would like to say that it is really important to me that this is contained in the Minutes: Mr Fuhrmann's cost has been reduced. I and my group assume that these deposit obligations - no matter how long they last; whether they are forever or not - should never lead to cost burdens in the form of a government coming and saying that in the future the deposit is also linked to a stamp mark or to the cost of 0.1% of the stock capital or any other fee. We really need to ensure - we as legislators - that there are never any mandatory fees in this area. In the interests of a practice-oriented legislation, I ask you all to observe these three points. ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e6949	The next speaker is Mrs Mag. Dr. Fekter. - Please, you have the floor.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e6967	Minister! Mr President. High house! The Company Law Change Act is a very dry matter, although it is one of the most extensive and thickest laws the Justice Committee has ever passed. I also believe that its importance for Austrian economic life is exceptional, although, of course, economics, tax advisers and experts will be most concerned with this law. The fact that the law will affect our economic life, you recognize that 86 000 GesmbHs will be affected, as well as all AGs and capital companies. It is guided by the principle of creditor protection, investor protection, especially for small investors, and fairness in the event of uneven power relationships, especially in the case of merger, division or transformation. In the interests of the protection of creditors – and this has already been mentioned by some previous speakers today – there should be improved transparency of balance sheets. A new obligation to publish allows for the first time an insight into business data about the company book. I do not agree with Mr Rosenstingl if he believes that this is just a shell that is not really used on the market. I myself have experienced in my company that asphalt contracts were still carried out in millions of height on a storage site, although three days later the bankruptcy was registered. Information and bank information could not help me at the time, because no data were available. I am convinced that, especially with regard to bank information and information in the future, this obligation to disclose more honest figures on the market, which reveal how the company actually does. I am going to pay tribute to a company that has either not submitted its balance sheets to the company's book for more than three or four years, or whose balance sheet data is such that I must fear that I will not get the money to carry out contracts in millions of unsecured contracts for such a company. I am therefore very glad with regard to the protection of creditors that we are doing this in accordance with EU law and that there are new publicity obligations that can also be imposed by punishment. This punishment, of course, is painful, especially in the case of small companies, but I think this is justified in terms of the economic damage that big pleas cause. I also think it justified to call on this nine-month-free woman to send the balance sheet to the company book, because it corresponds to an ordinary merchant that he has an overview of his figures. Small Gesmbhs have a simplified publicity obligation of their balance sheet. In particular, it is considered that those GesmbHs have two of the following three characteristics in the two-year period: 37 million Schilling balance sheet, 74 million Schilling sales and 50 employees. We know from experience that has been made in Germany, where there have been similar rules for many years, that the companies – such as the publicity of them – do not adhere to them. The EU has therefore initiated an appeal procedure in Germany and has invited the Federal Government in Germany to comply with the implementation of this Directive. In the opinion of Germany, it is pointed out that the German Federal Government represents the legal system that, if one fails to comply with this legislation, the limitation of liability falls. A damocles sword that Austria's managing director and owner of GesmbHs are supposed to keep in mind: If they fail to comply with these rules, they may be included in the liability and then the limited liability of a GesmbH no longer works. It is well-noticed yet, but it is a legal point that can be very well represented. In addition to the disclosure obligation with regard to creditor protection, for medium-sized companies – these are between the above-mentioned criteria and 150 million Schilling balance sheet total, 300 million Schilling turnover and 250 employees, from these figures one speaks of large corporations – as well as for large capital companies in the future, a audit report by an auditor is necessary. It must therefore be noted that not only the tax advisor must accept the balance sheet, but a balance sheet auditor must give a final confirmation note. As mentioned, this disclosure obligation can be imposed by fines of 50 000 S and another 50 000 S. I think this is justified because it serves to protect the market when it comes to greater transparency in this regard. With regard to the pleite wave we are currently experiencing, this punishment – if it appears very high for small Gesmbhs – is avoidable if you stick to it and send the balance sheet to the company's book within nine months. A clearer picture of the actual conditions and the economic situation of the companies will also be drawn in equity law, e.g. by limiting the acquisition of own shares by 5 percent; investor protection is a priority. Investor protection also serves to limit the exposure of own shares. Detailed accounting rules, such as a collection bid, depreciation arrangements and reserve definitions, complement the already in force accounting law and are intended to make Austrian balance sheets comparable in accordance with the EU directives and, on the other hand, to reflect the actual economic situation of the EU. On the principle of fairness in the face of uneven power relations, we are taking a new path here in Austria, which is different from the EU. We have incorporated a model into the law that regulates the review of exchange conditions in a completely new way. We are using a body to review which, on the one hand, draws up an opinion on the case-law, on the other hand, can also be used in general to resolve disputes. We know the third effect in such review procedures, that is to say, that there should not be a flow of proceedings, because everyone has to fight the right itself, but the outcome of the challenge on the exchange relationship should be a third effect for all. On the one hand, investor protection, on the other hand, also serves the company that there is rapid clarity as to how this fairness is to be applied in the event of uneven conditions of power, and that companies which are subject to mergers or transformations quickly know how their conditions are. Furthermore, this new model also provides for the possibility of deciding who may challenge. There we have gone a path towards a certain amount of capital or ownership in the nominal. You'll see how this model proves itself. It is intended to help prevent corruption or to prevent the powerful from being overly unfair in relation to small investors. It is also planned that a procedure which has already been initiated will also be concluded and not – I say now – by means of co-money surplus of the one who complains at first – one will try to calm down, but if the conditions are unclear, then the procedure should also be terminated. It is a very modern, new way which the European Union does not know to this extent. You can think of a typical Austrian based on great consensus. We'll see how he'll prove himself. In economic life, this law, which is very extensive, will certainly bring more transparency, more creditor protection, more investor protection and more fairness to the market. Thank you.

The EU framework implements all the trade and social directives that have not yet been transformed, thus completing the current implementation needs of EU legal standards in the field of justice. In order to achieve the principle of freedom of establishment, uniform legal standards are necessary in all Member States, in particular equivalent provisions of creditor and shareholder protection, in order to safeguard and maintain corporate capital. This is primarily for the approximation of the accounting law. The new provisions on the establishment, audit and disclosure of the annual accounts of all capital companies are intended to improve international comparability of individual and group financial statements. Ensuring comparable information on the capital, financial and income situation of a company will lead to greater clarity and transparency, both to better and earlier self-information on corporate governance as well as to the protection of shareholders and creditors, as well as to the company's staff. However, this also contributes to early detection and to avoiding insolvencies as far as possible. With regard to the issue of publication, I would like to point out that compliance with the obligation to publish the annual accounts in due time will be carried out on the basis of the company's book courts and that, in the interest of the interested parties, this disclosure will be entered and published in the same book by filing the company book. The ADV Directory provides the technical conditions for this to be done as effectively as possible. The annual list, addressed by a previous speaker, is no longer available. The imposition of a penalty, which I would like to say to calm down, is dependent on the first call for submission, then a threat to the penalty, and only then can a penalty be imposed. Ladies and gentlemen! In order to enable and facilitate companies to adapt their social structure to the needs of businesses, to the respective fiscal conditions and to the requirements of the larger market and thereby to strengthen their competitiveness, the re-establishment operations, taking into account the need for protection of the shareholders, in particular the overarching minority shareholders, have been thoroughly re-regulated. The new regulations in the Company Book Act on the electronic application or acceptance and transfer of applications for registration in the company's book by lawyers or notaries, as well as the related general obligation of lawyers in the legal order to provide technical institutions necessary for a reasonable party representation, are further steps on our way to the renewal of the judiciary through the use of modern information technology. In order to achieve the greatest possible rationalisation effects, it is not only the reform measures within the judiciary, that is to say in the courts, but also the willingness of the legal professions to take part in and participate in these modernisations. One thing can be seen as a fact: As in any large company, there is no maladministration, but I assume that the Minister of State and those responsible for it will eliminate these maladministrations. But in the vast majority of cases – I can say that with great joy – the procurements were carried out extremely correctly! I believe that this must be stressed positively here in the House. What this report from the Court of Auditors also shows from my point of view is the need to reform the procurement system in the Federal Army. Ladies and gentlemen! This reform is a command of the hour, is a particularly important project, and, as I believe, one of the priority projects in the next steps of the military reform. I only regret that for years we have only been talking about this. Federal Minister! Please do it at last! There is also an opportunity to realize this, because it is now mentioned in the Coalition Agreement. But I would like to say – but I would be pleased if it did not vote –: The news I hear is that I alone do not believe that this reform of procurement is actually and concretely implemented. Ladies and gentlemen! We have always called for a reform of procurement. From our point of view, it is really not a question of the federal army and the respective process coming to the public's criticism that there is a sharp political discussion about this, because the material conditions for such a discussion are not really given, nor are they brought into the discussion by those responsible. That is why I hope that the proposed reform will finally remove all these weaknesses. From our point of view, ladies and gentlemen, four areas must be addressed in particular: first, parliamentary consultation of all large-scale procurement projects. I can imagine that all projects over 200 million Schilling will be discussed and discussed either in the National Defence Committee or in the National Defence Council. For me, this is a question of transparency, which is also a question of political control of the budget implementation, but also of the concreteisation of the annual budget estimates. Ladies and gentlemen! All that Parliament is doing here every year in the framework of the budget, which decides on proposals, is not very meaningful if we look at it more closely. It is therefore useful and only legitimate to require that more concrete procurement projects be discussed in Parliament, the Committee or the Council of State Defense. Ladies and gentlemen! If this is possible in other democratic countries, it will also be possible in the Republic of Austria. If that does not harm the respective armed forces in other countries, then there will also be no harm to the German army for such advice in Parliament! Secondly, I call for a multiannual financing plan and also for a multiannual budget framework. Because one thing is clear: The safer and the longer a planning can be made, the cheaper, cheaper and more economical can also be purchased. Such a multiannual budget framework and financial plan are a very essential basis for planning. I believe that it is necessary to put it on the basis of the Federal Government's budget programme, so that security is given within the framework of planning, security within the framework of procurement, and therefore – and this also seems to me very important, ladies and gentlemen – the policy clearly states how much it is prepared to provide the Federal Government with financial resources so that policy can clearly tell to what extent and to what extent procurement can and should be carried out. That is why, as a member of the Council of State Defense, I also wrote a letter to Mr Bundeskanzler asking me to take up his deliberations on this item on the agenda of the Council of State Defense. Having heard Mr Gaál's speech today – I agree with him on this point – and since I also know the opinion of my colleagues from the Austrian People's Party and the Freedom Party, I am optimistic that we will be able to reach a sensible solution on this issue. The third point, Mr Federal Minister: It will be necessary to make internal structural improvements. We need clear decision-making skills. We need fast and unbureaucratic procedures, especially in the procurement sector, so that, if the policy is given the green light, these policies can also be implemented quickly. I regret that most of the arms policy objectives, as set out in the recommendation of the Council of State Defense on military integration, have not yet been implemented. This is due to the fact that we have too much bureaucracy in the procurement sector. Thirdly, therefore, it is important and necessary to improve internal procurement structures. The fourth point, which seems to me to be very important, is to build an industrial military cooperation, but not only with the Austrian industry, ladies and gentlemen, but also within the framework of the European Community, within the framework of the Western European Union, because this cooperation can make procurement for the German army much more favourable. Then there is a corresponding flow of information towards industry, towards the economy, and there is also the possibility of very useful and optimal links between the logistics of the German army and the industrial base. This serves both sides: on the one hand, the economy and on the other, the German army. Mr Wabl spoke very polemic about the question of procurement for the German army. One must also say clearly that arms are today high-tech products with a very wide spectrum also in the civil sector. Therefore, these procurements or the construction of a corresponding economy should not be eroded, but this may well also be a contribution to securing the location of Austria and to making it future-oriented. I can therefore conclude. It seems important to me that we attach more importance to the national economic component of procurement for the German army in politics, that instead of polemics, more seriousness is shown in the discussion. We need an objective discussion. We need transparency, because this means that security is more served in the interests of our country, and with it the funds are better used for the federal army. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e10772	Mr President. High house! Mr Gaál referred to me a question concerning the method of selection which the Court of Auditors has carried out in connection with the current examination. Deputies! I can tell you the following: ==References====External links== On 1 February 1995 the Court of Auditors examined all the matters raised by the Federal Ministry of Defence from 1 February 1995. In 1987, substantial contracts were made. The Court of Auditors' first task, after receiving this mandate, was to make a point of view of how many cases of procurement have taken place at all. We have come to the conclusion that there have been some 38 000 cases of procurement. It was quite clear that the Court of Auditors could only examine a fraction of these procurement cases in full. He therefore had to use a method of selection, one which is indisputable, because it would clearly have been possible, in some cases, to restrict some of the cases, to make the Court of Auditors accused of having chosen the "false" cases. With this in mind, the Court of Auditors very soon contacted university institutions, with the support of Professors Green and Mochty, who are also mentioned in the report. I would like to express my thanks again to these two professors of the Court of Auditors in plenary of the National Council, not only because they really gave us a very useful method, but also because they also worked for the Court of Auditors free of charge and not only sacrificed a day for the Court of Auditors – and thus also for the work that the Court of Auditors had to do for the National Council – but also for having been in contact with the Court of Auditors several times and having worked together with the Court of Auditors on this method. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] It was clear to the Court of Auditors that he could not only use value-dependent parameters in the selection, but also that he had to take value-independent parameters on the basis of his selection. The value-dependent parameters concerned in particular the amount of the order values. The value-independent parameters were diverse: On the one hand, it was important to consider whether these were new purchases or re-orders, on the other hand whether these were cases which were already the subject of criticism or treatment through the internal revision of the Ministry of State Defense, or, of course, that was particularly important for the Court of Auditors – whether they were cases which had already been discussed or treated in the past in the political field. This was clearly of particular importance to the Court of Auditors, because it has received the mandate of the National Council, from a high-level body. The Court of Auditors has therefore deliberately taken up those cases, particularly those which have already been subject to parliamentary questions or discussions in the National Council, the National Defence Council or the National Defence Committee. The Court of Auditors has given this particular importance in its assessment. In this context, Mr President-in-Office of the Council, he also raised the question of the importance of parliamentary questions. – Deputies! Let me give you some statistics. Of the 38 000 procurement cases I mentioned earlier, 162 were the subject of parliamentary questions. That's about 0.4 percent of all procurement cases. However, the Court of Auditors - I have already mentioned this - has given a much higher priority to procurement cases which were the subject of parliamentary questions than to just 0.4 percent. You can say: Around 10 percent of all procurement cases examined by the Court of Auditors were subject to parliamentary requests. Furthermore, five classes of risk have been identified, and we have listed them in the report, and a selection has been made from this by sampling. After the first partial report, we have undergone a review of this method, which proved to be quite desirable, namely whether it has proved successful – and we have come to the conclusion that we had no reason to take this method. This too – I may add – took place again in agreement and in cooperation with the university professors mentioned above. We see that we have chosen the right way of doing so, and we will therefore continue to do so. This method – it has shown in the past that the hope laid down in this method was quite true – will therefore also be applied to the future reports submitted by the Court of Auditors in the context of the performance of the National Council's overall mandate. In any event, I am convinced that this method will contribute to the satisfaction of the National Council's examinations which we are required to carry out.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e10866	Mr President. Mr President of the Court of Auditors! Ladies and gentlemen! At the same time, the Court of Auditors' report, which is the result of a special audit, expresses the fact that there has probably never been, even with certainty, a ministry in Austria, as comprehensively and intensively as was the case with the Federal Ministry of Defence. Of course, there are also different approaches in the course of an audit result. Let me briefly sum up what was essential in my assessment of this case. At the centre of the review was the acquisition of anti-terrorist weapons at the end of this last decade. The Court of Auditors report shall carry out verbatim report of proceedings: "According to a report by the General Force Inspector on the testing of the PAL systems, the State Defense Council recommended to the Federal Government in June 1989 the equipment of the Federal Government with armored arms of the Swedish company. In the same month, the Federal Government took a decision accordingly." From the point of view of the time, the members of the National Defence Council and the members of the Federal Government clearly felt sufficiently informed about all the foundations of the procurement. In fact, the Court's documents clearly show that there were performance requirements, such as the permeability or the safety of the damage. It is also clear that it was a competitive award and that the award was made only after an actual test had been carried out. At the same time, it is also noted that, according to the General Force Inspector, the technically best evaluation of the product was achieved at the time, for which there was also the supplement. The Court of Auditors' report notes, on the other hand, that certain requirements were not clearly understandable, for example as regards the time-limits as regards the documentation or the details of the implementation. There are some very important reasons for this. I would like to say this again: From today's point of view, it is inconceivable that seven years ago the Austrian federal army probably did not have a single steering force in the world, which has a range of 2,000 meters – that is because the State Treaty did not allow us to do so. It is therefore also understandable that, as is usual in all other cases and as can be expected, there was no compulsory specification in the formal preparation, therefore it is also quite understandable that all political aspects, namely the approval of the State Contracting Forces et cetera, should be taken into account once after the Second World War, in order to enable such a procurement. I am therefore seeking to look at this case of procurement in the light of the time. In addition, I would like to say the following: We will, of course, – and this has been the case since the beginning of 1990 – carry out all major procurements only on the basis of a duty booklet. Of course, according to the principle of competition, the four-eye principle, they will be controlled accordingly and sufficiently internally, and further reviews will also be carried out. In this sense, we are happy to accept the Court of Auditors' suggestions for improvements. It is likely that even in the future there or there minor errors, failures or misunderstandings will not be excluded. Where people act, mistakes are also made. But: The procedures are regulated, the control mechanisms are there. If we pay attention to this, then – I am convinced – in the best-tested place of the country in the future there will be procurements – even if there are so many criticisms from the outside as lobbyist reasons, namely because one wants to get a contract, in which one will find nothing challenging, but it will always prove that the processes work and that the result is to be called serious. ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e11033	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I was pleased to note that the adoption of the adaptations of Austrian company law to EU law this morning has taken an important step towards simplifying and increasing general legal certainty. Nevertheless, we Liberals believe that, as far as capital companies are concerned, Austrian company law would tolerate some further reforms. We want to open the debate on the number of Supervisory Board members in capital companies with this proposal. However, I do not want to hide the fact that there is certainly one point or the other that we should talk about, for example, extended liability provisions or the intensification of the liability of members of the Supervisory Board, because the existing practice is not particularly satisfactory. However, these suggestions have not yet been found in our current request. But there is no doubt that there is a willingness to discuss it. Ladies and gentlemen! The existing regulations provide for a restriction of 10 or 20 Supervisory Board functions for natural persons under the GmbH and Aktiengesetz. For cooperatives, no maximum limits have been reached in the law at all. Apart from the fact that, in the case of cooperatives, it is an unfair treatment against the legal form of the stock company or the Gesmbh, the exception for public-law representatives who are delegated to the supervisory councils of public limited companies and GesmbH, where this appears to be necessary in the interests of public-law bodies, is also a problem, so to speak, because it is clearly a discrimination against private mandates. That seems to us to be the first approach worthy of discussion. There is, however, a second point to be criticized. The existing publicity obligations of Supervisory Board mandates do not seem to us liberals to be transparent enough. It is extremely difficult for outsiders, but also for re-appointed members of the Supervisory Board, to determine how many such functions a new member has to be ordered and in which companies this is the case. In our opinion, however, society should know this. It has a right to quickly and unbureaucratic knowledge of it. In the case of cooperatives, as I said, the situation is completely opaque. I would like to present this to you on the basis of a small example. I know a respected person who holds the following Supervisory Board mandates: four mandates in stock companies, five mandates in GesmbHs – the companies have nothing to do with each other, so they are not group-related to each other – and in addition, this person exercises three Supervisory Board mandates in cooperatives; they are not small cooperatives, but large cooperatives, including a cooperative, and one of these cooperatives is in a more or less direct competitive relationship with one of the stock companies in which the person also exercises a mandate. In this case, the number of ten has been exceeded, a total of twelve mandates. On the part of the cooperatives, there is no knowledge of the functions of this Supervisory Board member elsewhere. We would, however, welcome it if there were to be complete transparency. I believe that this would also be necessary in the sense of equal treatment. Ladies and gentlemen! In the internal circle, we have considered in detail whether we need at all a regulation which provides for a ceiling on supervisory mandates, which is because it is important to weigh the number 10 against the number 20 in the public mandates. There is one way to say that we are creating an approximation, that is, ten and ten or 20 and 20 and there is the other possibility of abolishing the ceiling at all. We have therefore made a comparison as is usual in other countries, as this works there, and have also looked at the publicity obligations in other countries and have come to the very clear realization: We basically do not need this ceiling, however, with the limitation that we intensify the quality of the obligation to publish in Austria, because the shareholder and shareholder representative, the decision-makers in the general meetings, who then actually implements and executes the orders, should know very well for the benefit of society who is coming in and whether there are any competitive conditions. We believe that we can take account of these extended publicity obligations by publishing the appointment of a new Supervisory Board member in good time, for example in the "Official Journal to Vienna Newspaper", which is already common practice. So to speak, the cycle would be closed again. In line with the liberal principle of own responsibility, the capital company – and only it itself – will in future determine which and how many supervisory functions a new supervisory board member can perform. The legislator should withdraw from this scheme. Ladies and gentlemen! I would be pleased if the second reading would lead to a fruitful, interesting and constructive discussion about it. I'm counting on your participation and your constructive cooperation. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e11458	That's what we're going to do. ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e11485	Mr President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen. Please refer to our request: It's about housing property. The motivation to make such a request at all comes from the fact that there is a plurality of different forms of life – marriage, communities of life, single parents, singles, but also multigenerational families – and because we Greens believe that we should not close our eyes before it, but that concrete measures should be taken to take account of this diversity. Let us look at the figures of development, only in the field of living communities, which are, so to speak, comparable to a marriage – but these are couples who are not married –: This figure has risen sharply and has increased by 6 percent between 1984 and 1993. But this is just one of the life forms that we want to tailor with it. There are also other forms that are well understood of rising, one of them – I have already mentioned them – is the multigenerational family, the situation that mother or father wants to establish with daughter or son or grandmother with granddaughter or grandson's property. We can assume that housing ownership is becoming increasingly important in satisfying the housing needs; in this respect, the figures are even very much on the rise – from 1981 to 1991 by 36 percent. Property ownership will be increasingly important for various reasons, but certainly also for reasons of investment security for those who can afford to move in this direction in the long term. On the other hand, one must also say that outside the cities, where there are hardly any other possibilities to satisfy the housing need accordingly, housing ownership also plays a very important role. We have to deal with a housing property law which does not yet provide for the fact that partners are not married. In 1975, for the first time, it was possible for more than one person, i.e. spouses, two persons, to be registered together as owners of the land register, which is important in so far as it creates a number of rights, especially in the case of separation, death, that is, with regard to entry into housing property. However, there is no possibility in residential property, outside the situation of being married, together with another owner of housing. Our legislative initiative now aims to ensure that residential property can also be acquired together in a community of life and that this will end the de facto disadvantage of women in communities, which is the most common form in which this is concerned, that this can be achieved for those women who contribute financially to the acquisition and preservation of housing property, but who are not yet covered by the basics. It is perhaps still important to mention in the context of the fact that on another side of housing ownership this situation has long been in line, namely in the promotion of housing. Almost all federal housing promotion laws now recognise communities. In other words, in terms of the right to support housing, there is the possibility of applying as a community. One can even go so far as to say that the partner of life – that is the more common case – increases the claims of the owner of the dwelling – usually this is the man – without self-ownership. There is therefore also a disadvantage in considering housing property in connection with housing promotion. What we want out is to say: There are various forms of communities. We have presented a definition here: Compatriots, partners in life, are two persons equal to or different sexes, who call themselves consistent as such. We do not depend on the similarity of the situation. This is very essential, only because we want to take other forms of life into account with this legislative initiative. That's one. The other thing, which is essential, is that, in our proposal, we put investment security in the foreground, we put it before supply, because we do not want to make it equal to a Community of the same kind, but we are based on our motivation for this request. As a result, in our proposal for a law, we have regulated all possible possibilities: from entering housing property in the event of death, when separation, when the community of life is dissolved. Yes, even in cases where urgent housing needs are claimed, we have regulated what will then enter into force. We have prepared the proposal accordingly, very comprehensive, very precise, and we are really trying to take into account every detail that could occur in such a situation. I would ask you, ladies and gentlemen of the other political groups, to discuss this very thoroughly and to discuss it with us in committee, to see this as an equalization in a small area, a situation which is not only de facto, as I have said, but which, if we start from the construction of housing, is already re-adjusted there or there, but where the real area of this property is missing. I would ask you, in this debate, to really take into account the plurality of the forms of life to which we are concerned, and also the corresponding generosity in the sense that we have defined and presented it here, namely that we are not concerned with living together of the same kind, but that two people – no matter what gender and any age – want to establish joint ownership of dwellings. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-05-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00025_d7e11573	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! The present draft law provides for the equality of living persons between the same and different sexes with spouses limited to the field of housing property law. In this context, a definition of the same or different sexes is created exclusively and punctually for the field of housing property law, whose relative indifference and formlessness I believe is immense, but at the same time is intended to provide the basis for permanent, long-term criminal rights. Ladies and gentlemen! Although the general social policy objective of approaching or comparing the often occurring, modern factic communities of life to the more strictly formal marriage communities seems to be quite sympathetic and supportive, I believe that the legal and legal systems chosen here are an inappropriate: The approximation of the legal and economic frameworks of communities of different traditions and permanence by means of a time-consuming law to solve a lot more problems than they can solve. This begins with the Lapidar questions that inevitably follow this discussion: Can a person have several companions at the same time? Can a married person call one or more persons the same or different sex as their partners? This unrestrictedness and formlessness is also in a clear but completely uncommented contradiction to the definition of the living companions in § 14 para. 3 of the Law on Rental Law, which serves as the basis for the right of entry of the companion in the death of the former tenant. In addition to the most serious inadequacy of the proposal for a law which has been introduced here in connection with the definition of life-mates, the proposal also contains some provisions with non-practicable rules, with only an example of the decision-making when the community is repealed as primary decision-making ceilings. Only these two examples prove that there is an attempt to stoop the horse at the tail with completely inadequate means. This also draws the attention of a benevolently critical viewer from the many very interesting and recognized solutions, which could be integrated into an overall concept of social-politically well-appointed and desirable reform of the right of persons and persons to stand. In my opinion, all the equality sought by non-marital communities with the marriage communities in the area of housing, social rights or inheritance rights can only be usefully developed if a widely accepted legal definition of the community of persons is created. If the same or different sexes of this community can also become a common carrier of legal rights, a minimum degree of legally binding formalisation of that community will have to be required, without all the living, social or inheritance regulations that are based on it would then be completely impracticable. In addition, ladies and gentlemen, I should like to remind you that legal harmonisation, involving non-marital communities, has completely exceeded its goal for so long or constitutes an alibi project, as long as it does not even result in legal equal treatment for married communities in all areas of national law. For example, the Kleingarten Act – we have already talked about it – excludes a common lease right of spouses, which would primarily serve the protection of the economic interests of married women, so that thousands of spouses, especially in Vienna, cannot have a common right of use at their homes, often used as their main residence all year round. I therefore advocate a path of consistent action, legal and economic protection of women in all forms of society. Let us begin, for example, with a revision of the Small Garden Act, which also allows spouses as small garden tenants, and then gradually devote ourselves to a systematic legalization of non-marital communities. Persons living in communities are entitled to legal certainty in the joint design of their housing. It is precisely this legal certainty that my predecessor's unexposed motion for a law cannot guarantee. [[Abg. Dr. Hazelsteiner: We can make a better proposal!]] The housing property, which is designed in the long term as a criminal law, is far less suitable for secure housing of living communities which are not necessarily to be received for a long time than for example the rental right.
